L1234: ## 2.4. Conclusion
L1235: The primary analysis demonstrated the significantly lower mean of Drug Liking Emax scores produced by both doses of KP415 prodrug compared to that by 80 mg Focalin XR with a test margin of 10 points (p\le 0.0085). However, the study failed to demonstrate no significant mean difference in maximum liking between each dose of KP415 prodrug and placebo (p\ge 0.0567). The study was validated by the significantly larger mean produced by 80 mg Focalin XR than that by placebo with a test margin 15 (p&lt;0.0001).
L1236: The results from the analysis on the secondary endpoints, Overall Drug Liking Emax and Take Drug Again Emax, demonstrated significant difference in means between 80 mg Focalin XR and placebo (p\le 0.0438). However, the results did not support the findings in the primary analysis for the comparisons between each dose of KP415 prodrug and 80 mg Focalin XR (p\ge 0.0873). It was because 11 (24.4%) and 10 (22.2%) subjects in some degree overall disliked 80 mg Focalin XR and did not want to take 80 mg Focalin XR again, respectively. The means of Overall Drug Liking Emax and Take Drug Again Emax were 63.0 and 64.6 produced by 80 mg Focalin XR, respectively, and the means produced by both doses of KP415 prodrug were within [56.9, 58.5] for both secondary endpoints. The descriptive statistics on the exploratory measures (Any Effects VAS, Bad Effects VAS, Good Effects VAS, Alertness/Drowsiness VAS, and High VAS) and their Emax scores supported the findings from the primary analysis for these comparisons.
L1237: The comparisons between 60 mg phentermine and each dose of KP415 prodrug were prespecified as exploratory comparisons. These comparisons showed that both doses of KP415 prodrug produced means significantly smaller than 60 mg phentermine for the primary and secondary endpoints (p\le 0.0195) except the comparison between 60 mg phentermine and 240 mg KP415 for Drug Liking Emax. Note that this comparison used a test margin of 10 points (In general, sponsors used a test margin of zero for the comparison between a schedule IV positive control and test drug).
L1238: The p-value of the test was 0.0664, and the lower confidence limit of this comparison was 9.6. The significantly larger mean produced by 60 mg phentermine than that by placebo with a test margin of 15 points validated the exploratory comparisons (p=0.0032). The descriptive statistics of exploratory endpoints supported that both doses of KP415 prodrug had less abuse potential compared to 60 mg phentermine.
L1239: In conclusion, Study KP415-a01 demonstrated that with oral administration 120 and 240 mg doses of KP415 prodrug had less abuse potential compared to not only 80 mg Focalin XR but also 60 mg phentermine. However, the study did not demonstrate that both doses of KP415 prodrug had no abuse potential compared to placebo.
L1240: ## 3. Review report on Study KP415-a02
L1241: ## 3.1. Overview
L1242: Study KP415-a02 was a phase 1, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, single-dose, 3-Way crossover study to determine the abuse potential and pharmacokinetics of 80 mg KP415 Prodrug API compared with 40 mg of d-Methylphenidate API after intranasal administration in healthy, nondependent, recreational stimulant users.
L1243: ## 3.1.1. Objectives of the study
L1244: ## Primary Objectives
L1245: - To assess the abuse potential of KP415 prodrug API relative to d-MPH API when administered intranasally in healthy, nondependent, recreational stimulant users.
- To assess the PK of intact KP415 and KP415-derived d-MPH after a single IN dose of KP415 prodrug API in healthy, nondependent, recreational stimulant users.
L1246: ## Secondary Objectives
L1247: - To assess the safety and tolerability of a single IN dose of KP415 prodrug API in healthy, nondependent, recreational stimulant users.
- To assess the PK of l-MPH (if applicable) and ritalinic acid after a single IN dose of KP415 prodrug API in healthy, nondependent, recreational stimulant users.
L1248: ## 3.1.2. Study design
L1249: The overall study design is presented in Figure 1. The study consisted of a Screening Period, an in-clinic Drug Discrimination Phase, a Treatment Phase, and a Follow-up visit, as follows:
L1250: - *Screening Period:* Subjects underwent a screening period up to 28 days prior to entering the Drug Discrimination Phase of the study.
- *Drug Discrimination Phase:* Subjects who successfully completed the screening period returned to the clinic to complete the Drug Discrimination Phase. The Drug Discrimination test ensured that subjects were able to differentiate between the effects of a single dose of d-MPH API and placebo administered intranasally. Subjects who successfully completed the Drug Discrimination Phase remained as inpatients to enter the Treatment Phase. The Drug Discrimination Phase was a double-blind, IN, single-dose, 2-treatment, 2-period, 2-sequence, randomized, crossover design. Subjects received single IN doses of the following treatments separated by a 48-hour washout period:
L1251: - Treatment X: 40 mg d-MPH API mixed with 40 mg microcrystalline cellulose (MCC)
- Treatment Y: 80 mg matching placebo powder
L1252: All subjects were required to fast for at least 8 hours prior to each dose until approximately 4 hours after each dose. Abuse potential assessments were performed at different times after the administration of study drug. Safety assessments were performed throughout and at the end of the Drug Discrimination Phase. A subject was eligible for the Treatment Phase only if all the eligibility criteria for the Drug Discrimination Phase, based on the drug effects and tolerability assessments, were met. The washout period between the last dose of the study
L1253: drug in the Drug Discrimination Phase and the first dose of study drug in the Treatment Phase was approximately 70 to 72 hours.

- **Treatment Phase:** After subjects completed and passed the Drug Discrimination Phase, they were eligible to enter into the Treatment Phase. The Treatment Phase was a double-blind, IN, single-dose, 3-treatment, 3-period, 6-sequence, randomized, crossover design separated by a minimum 96-hour washout period, with the following treatments:Treatment A: 80 mg KP415 prodrug API
- Treatment B: 40 mg d-MPH API
- Treatment C: matching placebo powder
All subjects were required to fast for at least 8 hours prior to each dose until approximately 4 hours after each dose. Abuse potential measures and PK were collected at different times after the administration of study drug. Safety assessments were performed throughout and at the end of the Treatment Phase.- Follow-up visit: 7\pm 2 days after administration of the last dose of study drug in the Treatment Phase, subjects returned to the research clinic for a Follow-up visit to evaluate safety parameters.
L1254: The following chart shows the overall study design. It is Figure 1 in the study report.
L1255: Figure 1 Overall Study Design
L1256: API=active pharmaceutical ingredient, MCC=microcrystalline cellulose
L1257: ## Eligibility Criteria to Enter Treatment Phase
L1258: Drug Liking scores and other HAP PD measures in the Drug Discrimination Phase were used to determine whether subjects could discriminate between d-MPH API and placebo, and, in combination with safety and tolerability data, whether subjects were eligible to continue in the Treatment Phase, according to the following criteria:
L1259: - Peak score (E_{max}) of at least 65 points for d-MPH API on the Drug Liking visual analog scale (VAS) and at least 15 points higher in response to d-MPH API vs placebo response (so that subjects have a minimal response to d-MPH API, outside the placebo response range).
- Placebo response was in the range of 40 to 60 points (inclusive) on the Drug Liking VAS (so that subjects neither endorsed liking nor disliking of placebo).
- Acceptable overall responses to d-MPH API and placebo on all other HAP PD measures, as judged by the investigator or designee.
- Acceptable safety and tolerability after IN administration of the d-MPH API dose.
- Subject was able to insufflate at least 95% of the drug product (d-MPH API and placebo).
L1260: The principal investigator, in collaboration with the sponsor and medical monitor, evaluated these criteria for individual subjects and decided whether subjects were eligible for the Treatment Phase. Subjects who did not meet these requirements were withdrawn from further study participation.
L1261: A subject was eligible for the Treatment Phase only if all the eligibility criteria were met at the end of the Drug Discrimination Phase. On a case-by-case basis, a subject who met all of the above criteria, with the exception of sporadic erroneous responses, may have been permitted into the Treatment Phase, provided that the investigator or designee determined that the subject's response was made in error and that the subject could be successfully retrained for the Treatment Phase regarding proper completion of that assessment.
L1262: ## **3.1.3. Study subjects**
L1263: A total of 154 subjects were screened. A total of 68 subjects were randomized to the Qualification Phase. Of these 68 subjects, 50 subjects passed Qualification Phase and 49 subjects were randomized to the Treatment Phase, and 45 subjects completed the study. The following chart of disposition of subjects is Figure 3 in the study report.
L1264: Figure 3 Disposition of Subjects
L1265: Source: Essential Logs, Investigator Site File
L1266: Note: This is Figure 3 in the study report.
L1267: ## 3.1.4. Endpoints
L1268: - Primary endpointDrug Liking Emax
- Secondary endpointsTake Drug Again Emax
- Overall Drug Liking Emax
- Exploratory EndpointsEmax for High, Good Effects, Bad Effects, Any Effects, and Drowsiness/Alertness VAS scores, and for ARCI-A and ARCI-BG scores
- Ease of Nasal Insufflation VAS (score at post-dose assessment)
- TEmax and area under the effect curves (AUEs) for Drug Liking
- Descriptive statistics onlyPercent reduction in Drug Liking Emax between test, control, and placebo
- TEmax for High, Good Effects, Bad Effects, Any Effects, and Drowsiness/Alertness VAS scores, and ARCI-A and ARCI-BG scores
- Emin, TEmin for Drug Liking
L1269: ## 3.1.5. Statistical methodologies used in the Sponsor's analyses
L1270: The statistical methodologies used in the Sponsor's analyses in this study are the same as those in Study KP415-A01. See section 2.1.5.
L1271: ## 3.1.6. Sponsor's Summary and Conclusions on Pharmacodynamic study
L1272: ## Primary analysis
L1273: - The median of the difference in Drug Liking VAS Emax between d-MPH API 40 mg and placebo was 45.0 and the lower bound of the 95% CI was 41.0, which was greater than the margin of 15 (P&lt;0.0001); therefore, the null hypothesis was rejected, and the validity of the study was confirmed.
- The mean difference in Drug Liking VAS Emax between d MPH API 40 mg vs KP415 prodrug API 80 mg was 22.3 and the lower bound of the 95% CI was 17.3, which was greater than the margin of 10 (P&lt;0.0001); therefore, the null hypothesis was rejected. These results demonstrated that compared to d-MPH API 40 mg, KP415 prodrug API 80 mg (equimolar dose of d-MPH) was significantly less liked by recreational stimulant users.
- The mean difference in Drug Liking VAS Emax between KP415 prodrug API 80 mg vs placebo was 19.9 and the upper limit of the 95% CI was 24.6, which was greater than the margin of 11 (P=0.999); therefore, the null hypothesis was accepted. These results indicate that the subjective drug liking of KP415 prodrug API 80 mg was not statistically similar to placebo.
L1274: ## Secondary analysis
L1275: For secondary endpoints of Overall Drug Liking and Take Drug Again, Emax values for KP415
L1276: prodrug API 80 mg were significantly lower than for d-MPH API 40 mg (equimolar dose of d-MPH) yet significantly higher than for placebo. These findings indicate that subjects liked KP415 prodrug less overall and would be less willing to take KP415 prodrug again when compared to d-MPH; however, subjects liked KP415 prodrug more overall and would be more willing to take KP415 prodrug again when compared to placebo.
L1277: ## Exploratory analysis
L1278: - The profile of significant differences for the exploratory endpoints mirrored that of the primary and secondary endpoints, where the rank order of positive abuse-related effects was d-MPH API &gt; KP415 prodrug API &gt; placebo.
- For the majority of measures assessing positive drug effects (Good Effects and High), stimulant-like effects (ARCI-A, ARCI-BG), Drowsiness/Alertness VAS) and other effects (Any Effects VAS), KP415 prodrug API engendered Emax values that were significantly lower than d-MPH and significantly higher than placebo.
- Negative drug effects were low overall. Bad Effects VAS Emax was significantly higher for d-MPH API 40 mg and KP415 prodrug API 80 mg compared with placebo; however, bad effects were not significantly different between the active treatments.
- KP415 prodrug API 80 mg was reported to be significantly more difficult to insufflate than both d-MPH API 40 mg and placebo, as assessed on the Ease of Insufflation scale.
L1279: ## Conclusions
L1280: Overall, the PD results indicate that positive subjective effects of IN KP415 prodrug API 80 mg were significantly lower compared with d-MPH API 40 mg and significantly higher compared with placebo. These findings suggest that KP415 prodrug API has lower abuse potential than d-MPH API when administered by the IN route, but is not devoid of abuse potential.

L1289: ## 3.3.1. Insufflation
L1290: All subjects, including those who discontinued early, were able to completely insufflate the study treatments. However, 80 mg KP415 prodrug API was reported to be significantly more difficult to insufflate than both 40 mg d-MPH API and placebo, as assessed on the Ease of Insufflation scale. L1304: ## 3.3.1.2. Statistical testing
L1305: For examining the abuse potential of KP415 prodrug with nasal administration, the reviewer studied the following comparisons:
L1306: - 40 mg d-MPH API versus placebo (study validation)
- 40 mg d-MPH API versus 80 mg KP415 prodrug API (relative abuse potential)
- 80 mg KP415 prodrug API versus P (absolute abuse potential)
L1307: Corresponding hypotheses for these comparisons on Drug Liking Emax are listed below:
L1308: - H_0: \mu_{d-MPH} - \mu_P \le 15 vs. H_a: \mu_{d-MPH} - \mu_P &gt; 15.
- H_0: \mu_{d-MPH} - \mu_{KP415} \le 10 vs. H_a: \mu_{d-MPH} - \mu_{KP415} &gt; 10,
- H_0: \mu_{KP415} - \mu_P \ge 11 vs. H_a: \mu_{KP415} - \mu_P &lt; 11.
L1309: The same statistical model and analysis methods as those used in Study KP415-a01 were used in this study. The normality assumption of the model was not satisfied. The p-value of the W-test on residuals was less than 0.0001. The normality of the paired differences was examined. L1315: The results from the primary analysis show that for Drug Liking Emax,
L1316: - the median difference between 40 mg d-MPH API and placebo was significantly larger than 15 points (p&lt;0.0001). Thus, the study was validated;
L1317: 2) 80 mg KP415 prodrug API produced significantly smaller mean than 40 mg d-MPH API with a test margin of 10 points (p&lt;0.0001);
3) The median difference between 80 mg KP415 prodrug API and placebo was not significantly smaller than 11 points (p=0.9980).

L1349: ## 3.4. Conclusion
L1350: The primary analysis demonstrated that for Drug Liking Emax, the mean difference between 40 mg d-MPH API and 80 mg KP415 prodrug API was significantly larger than 10 points (p&lt;0.0001). However, the study failed to demonstrate median difference between 40 mg KP415 prodrug API and placebo significantly smaller than 11 points (p=0.9998). The median difference between 40 mg d-MPH API and placebo significantly greater than 15 points validated the study (p&lt;0.0001).
L1351: The results from the secondary analysis showed that for both Overall Drug Liking Emax and Take Drug Again Emax, the median difference between 40 mg d-MPH API and placebo, the mean difference between 40 mg d-MPH API and 80 mg KP415 prodrug API, and the median difference between 80 mg KP415 prodrug API and placebo were significantly greater than zero (p\le 0.0027).
L1352: The descriptive statistics for the exploratory measures (Any Effects VAS, Bad Effects VAS, Good Effects VAS, Alertness/Drowsiness VAS, High VAS) and their Emax scores supported the findings from the primary and secondary analyses.
L1353: All subjects, including those who discontinued early, were able to completely insufflate the study treatments. The means for Ease of Nasal Insufflation at 5 minutes post-dose were 19.1, 65.8 and 6.9 for 40 mg d-MPH API, 80 mg KP415 prodrug API and placebo, respectively. The standard deviation was larger produced by 80 mg KP415 prodrug API (32.0) compared to those by both 40 mg d-MPH API (23.5) and placebo (18.8). The correlation of Ease of Nasal Insufflation at 5 minutes post-dose and Drug Liking Emax and that of Ease of Nasal Insufflation at 5 minutes post-dose and Take Drug Again Emax produced by 80 mg KP415 prodrug API were small (0.1252 and -0.2598, respectively), which indicates that the difficulty of nasal insufflation produced by 80 mg KP415 prodrug API may not be the main reason for reducing the abuse potential for 80 mg KP415 prodrug API compared to 40 mg d-MPH API.
L1354: In conclusion, Study KP415-a02 demonstrated that with intranasal administration 80 mg KP415 prodrug API had less abuse potential than 40 mg d-MPH API and had greater abuse potential compared to placebo.
L1355: ## 4. Review report on Study KP415-a03
L1356: ## 4.1. Overview
L1357: KP415-a03 was a Phase 1, double-blind, single-dose, 3-treatment, 3-Period, 6-sequence, randomized crossover study to compare the abuse potential and pharmacokinetics of KP415 API with d-methylphenidate API administered intravenously in healthy, nondependent, recreational stimulant users.
L1358: ## 4.1.1. Objectives of the study
L1359: ## Primary objectives
L1360: ## Cohort 1
L1361: - To determine the optimal dose of d-MPH API based on tolerability and HAP pharmacodynamic (PD) assessments after a single IV injection in nondependent, recreational stimulant users.
L1362: ## Cohort 2
L1363: - To assess the abuse potential of KP415 API after a single IV injection in nondependent, recreational stimulant users.
- To assess the PK of intact KP415, KP415-derived d-MPH, l-MPH (if applicable), and ritalinic acid after a single IV injection of KP415 API in nondependent, recreational stimulant users.
L1364: ## Secondary objective for Cohort 2
L1365: To Assess the safety of KP415 API after a single IV injection in nondependent, recreational stimulant users.
L1366: *Reviewer's comment: This review focused on the primary objectives for cohort 2 in the Part B of the study.*
L1367: ## 4.1.2. Study design for Part B
L1368: Part B (Cohort 2) consisted of a Screening Period, a Drug Discrimination Phase (Qualification Phase), a Treatment Phase and a Follow-Up Visit, as follows:
L1369: Screening Period: Subjects underwent a screening period up to 35 days prior to entering the Drug Discrimination Phase.
L1370: *Drug Discrimination Phase (Qualification Phase):* Subjects who successfully completed the Screening Period returned to the clinic to complete the Drug Discrimination Phase (Qualification Phase). The Drug Discrimination Test was performed to ensure that subjects could differentiate between the effects of a single dose of d-MPH API and placebo administered by IV injection. Subjects who successfully completed the Drug Discrimination Test remained as inpatients to enter the Treatment Phase. The Drug Discrimination Phase was a double-blind, single-dose, IV, 2 treatment, 2-period, 2-sequence, randomized crossover design. Subjects received single IV doses of
L1371: the following treatments separated by a minimum 24-hour washout period:
L1372: - Treatment X: d-MPH API 15 mg, IV.
- Treatment Y: Matching placebo, IV
L1373: HAP PD assessments were performed at different times after the administration of study drug. Safety assessments were performed throughout and at the end of the Drug Discrimination Phase. A subject was eligible for the Treatment Phase only if all eligibility criteria for Drug Discrimination based on the HAP pharmacodynamic assessments were met.
L1374: *Treatment Phase:* After subjects passed and completed the Drug Discrimination Phase, they were eligible to enter into the Treatment Phase. The Treatment Phase was a double-blind, single-dose, IV, 3-treatment, 3-period, 6-sequence, randomized crossover design, with the following treatments:
L1375: - Treatment A: KP415 API 30 mg, IV (molar equivalent for d-MPH to d-MPH API 15 mg dose; KP415 API – Test Product)
- Treatment B: d-MPH API 15 mg, IV (Control or Reference Product)
- Treatment C: Matching placebo IV formulation.
L1376: HAP PD assessments and PK samples were collected at different times after the administration of study drug. Safety assessments were performed throughout and at the end of the Treatment Phase.
L1377: *Follow-Up Visit:* 7 \pm2 days after administration of the last dose of study drug in the Treatment Phase, subjects attended a Follow-Up Visit to have safety parameters evaluated.
L1378: ## 4.1.3. Abuse potential measures and endpoints of interest
L1379: The PD measures of interest in the Treatment Phase were Drug Liking, Take Drug Again, Overall Drug Liking, Feeling High, Good Effects, Bad Effects, Any Effects, and Alertness/Drowsiness VAS scores, as well as ARCI-A and ARCI-BG scores.
L1380: The primary PD endpoint was Drug Liking VAS Emax.
L1381: The secondary endpoints in the Treatment Phase were as follows:
L1382: Drug Liking VAS (percent reduction in Drug Liking VAS Emax; time of occurrence of Emax [TEmax]; minimum effect [Emin]; time of occurrence of Emin [TEmin]; and area under the effect versus time curve from time 0 to time t [AUE0-t], where t includes all times post-dosing at which effects were measured)
L1383: Global Effects: Take Drug Again VAS (Emax: key secondary endpoint) and Overall Drug Liking VAS (Emax and Emin)
L1384: Positive effects: Feeling High VAS and Good Effects VAS (Emax, TEmax, AUEs)
L1385: Negative effect: Bad Effects VAS (Emax, TEmax, AUEs)
L1386: Other effects: Any Effects VAS (Emax, TEmax, AUEs)
L1387: Stimulant effects: Alertness/Drowsiness VAS, Addiction Research Center Inventory Amphetamine scale (ARCI-A), and ARCI Benzedrine Group scale (ARCI-BG) (Emax, TEmax, Emin, TEmin, AUEs, as applicable)
L1388: ## 4.1.4. Study subjects
L1389: A total of 199 subjects were screened for Part B, five of whom were originally screened for Part A, but were reconsented for Part B. Of these, 69 eligible subjects were randomized to the Drug Discrimination Phase. A total of 60 (87.0%) subjects completed the Drug Discrimination Phase, and 9 (13.0%) were discontinued. Five (7.2%) subjects were discontinued due to AEs, and 4 (5.8%) subjects were discontinued for “other” reasons, including family emergency (1 subject), noncompliance (1 subject), subject request (1 subject), and investigator decision due to event of elevated blood pressure (1 subject). Of the 60 subjects who completed the Drug Discrimination Phase, 21 subjects did not meet eligibility criteria to enter the Treatment Phase (Appendix 16.2.2.2.2 in the report), two subjects withdrew consent, four subjects were discontinued early, and two subjects were discontinued as enrollment was met. All subjects were included in the Qualification Safety Population.
L1390: A total of 31 subjects were randomized to the Treatment Phase. Thirty (96.8%) subjects completed the study, and 1 (3.2%) subject was discontinued for “other” reasons (family emergency). All subjects were included in the Safety Population and the 30 completers were included in the Completers Population and PP Population.
L1391: ## 4.1.5. Statistical methodologies used for the primary and key secondary endpoints
L1392: A mixed-effects analysis of covariance (ANCOVA) model for a crossover study was used to compare PD endpoints (e.g., Emax, Emin, AUEs) between treatments. The model included treatment, period and treatment sequence as fixed effects, and baseline (pre-dose) measurement as a covariate where applicable, and subject nested within sequence as a random effect. A first-order direct carryover effect from the treatment of previous period was included in the model if it was found to be significant at the 25% level. For the first treatment period, KP415 API was used for all subjects and all PD endpoints. For endpoints analyzed using the mixed effects model, least squares (LS) means, along with 95% confidence intervals (CIs) (or 90% CIs, for the secondary endpoint comparisons of KP415 API 30 mg vs. placebo), were provided for each treatment, and LS mean differences and associated CIs were provided for each pairwise comparison.
L1393: For all endpoints, the assumption of normality that is required for the ANCOVA model was examined using the Shapiro-Wilk Normality test on the residuals from the mixed-effect model. For endpoints that did not meet the normal distribution assumption, the differences between treatments were evaluated for symmetric distribution. In case it was found to be symmetric, a paired t-test was performed. Otherwise, the Friedman's method was used to test the overall treatment effect, and the Sign test was used to compare the pairwise treatment differences. The median differences and associated two-sided CI were estimated using the method of Hodges-Lehman. The derived parameters TEmax and TEmin parameters were analyzed using the non-parametric method.
L1394: ## *Primary and Key Secondary Endpoints*
L1395: The primary analyses were based on testing the hypotheses below for Drug Liking VAS Emax, with margins defined as follows: \delta_1 = 15 points, \delta_2 = 10 points, and \delta_3 = 11 points, and using one-sided 95% CIs (Due to certain limitations of conducting one-sided analyses in SAS, a two sided test was performed
L1396: to calculate 90% CIs if a primary analysis required a paired t-test or Sign test. The resulting lower limit [superiority test] or upper limit [noninferiority test] of the two-sided 90%CI was then compared with the respective margin and the p-value was divided by 2 to determine statistical significance relative to \alpha=0.05 consistent with a one-sided test with a one-sided 95% CI). The tests were conducted sequentially in the order listed below, and thus, no multiplicity testing was needed. The key secondary endpoint, Take Drug Again VAS Emax was analyzed in the same manner.
L1397: ## *Secondary Endpoints*
L1398: Secondary endpoint comparisons for d-MPH API versus placebo and d-MPH API versus KP415 API were performed as two-sided hypothesis tests at a significance level of \alpha=0.05 and reported with 95% CI. Secondary endpoint comparisons for KP415 API vs placebo were performed as two-sided hypothesis tests at a significance level of \alpha=0.10 and reported with 90% CI. The larger \alpha in the latter comparison reduces the Type II error (decreasing the probability of falsely assuming KP415 API and placebo have similar effects), and increases the power of this test.
L1399: ## 4.1.6. Sponsor's Pharmacodynamic Results
L1400: - The mean difference in Drug Liking VAS Emax (primary endpoint) between d-MPH API 15 mg and placebo was 30.5 and the lower bound of the 90% CI was greater than 15 (\delta_1; p&lt;0.001); therefore, the null hypothesis was rejected and the validity of the study was confirmed. The median difference in Drug Liking VAS Emax between d-MPH API 15 mg vs KP415 API 30 mg was 29.0 and the lower bound of the 90% CI was greater than 10 (\delta_2; p=0.001); therefore, the null hypothesis was rejected. These results demonstrate that compared to d-MPH API 15 mg, KP415 API 30 mg (equimolar dose of d-MPH) was significantly less liked by recreational stimulant users. The median difference in Drug Liking VAS Emax between KP415 API 30 mg vs placebo was 0.5 and the upper limit of the 90% CI was less than 11 (\delta_3; p=0.001); therefore, the null hypothesis was rejected. These results suggest that the subjective drug liking of KP415 API 30 mg was similar to that of placebo.
- The mean difference in Take Drug Again VAS Emax (key secondary endpoint) between d-MPH API 15 mg and placebo was 49.1 and the lower limit of the 95% CI was 35.2, which was greater than 15 (\delta_1; p&lt;0.001); therefore, the null hypothesis was rejected, indicating that subjects were more willing to take d-MPH API 15 mg again compared with placebo. The mean difference between d-MPH API 15 mg and KP415 API 30 mg was 43.1 and the lower limit of the 95% CI was 29.2, which was greater than 10 (\delta_2; p&lt;0.001); therefore, subjects were significantly less willing to take KP415 API 30 mg compared with d-MPH API 15 mg. The mean difference between KP415 API 30 mg and placebo was 6.0 and the upper limit of the 95% CI was 19.9, which was greater than 11 (\delta_3; p=0.275). Since the CI contained 11 (\delta_3), the null hypothesis was not rejected, indicating that subjects were more willing to take KP415 API 30 mg again compared with placebo. However, a median difference of 6 points on a unipolar 100-point VAS is small and may not be clinically meaningful.
- For the majority of derived Emax, Emin, and AUEs for measures of global effects, positive effects, and other effects (other secondary endpoints), d-MPH API 15 mg had statistically
L1401: significantly higher values compared with both KP415 API 30 mg and placebo, and there was no statistical difference between KP415 API 30 mg and placebo. Cumulative measures Drug Liking VAS AUE0-7hr and AUE0-12hr were statistically higher for KP415 API 30 mg as compared with placebo (p=0.099), but overall, KP415 API 30 mg had effects similar to placebo.

L1421: ## 4.3.2.2. Statistical testing
L1422: For examining the abuse potential of 30 mg KP415 API through IV administration, the reviewer studied the following comparisons:
L1423: - 15 mg d-MPH API versus Placebo (study validation)
- 15 mg d-MPH API versus 30 mg KP415 API (relative abuse potential)
- 30 mg KP415 API versus Placebo (absolute abuse potential)
L1424: Corresponding hypotheses for these comparisons on the primary endpoint, Drug Liking Emax, are listed below:
L1425: - H_0: \mu_{d-MPH} - \mu_P \le 15 vs. H_a: \mu_{d-MPH} - \mu_P &gt; 15
- H_0: \mu_{d-MPH} - \mu_{KP415} \le 10 vs. H_a: \mu_{d-MPH} - \mu_{KP415} &gt; 10
- H_0: \mu_{KP415} - \mu_P \ge 11 vs. H_a: \mu_{KP415} - \mu_P &lt; 11
L1426: The significance level for each test is 5%.
L1427: The same statistical model and analysis method as Studies KP415-a01 and KP415-a02 were used in this study.
L1428: The normality assumption of the model was not satisfied for Drug Liking Emax. The p-value of the W-test on residuals was 0.0009. The normality of the paired differences was examined. Table 16 provides the skewness and p-value of the W test for the paired differences for Drug Liking Emax.

L1447: ## 4.3.3.2. Statistical testing
L1448: Based on the protocol prespecified analysis, the analysis for the key secondary endpoint, Take Drug Again Emax, was planned the same as that for the primary endpoint, Drug Liking Emax. However, the sponsor did not take unipolar visual analog scale of this measure into account. The prespecified hypotheses were:
L1449: - H_0: \mu_{d-MPH} - \mu_P \le 15 vs. H_a: \mu_{d-MPH} - \mu_P &gt; 15
- H_0: \mu_{d-MPH} - \mu_{KP415} \le 10 vs. H_a: \mu_{d-MPH} - \mu_{KP415} &gt; 10
- H_0: \mu_{KP415} - \mu_P \ge 11 vs. H_a: \mu_{KP415} - \mu_P &lt; 11
L1450: The significance level for each test was 5%.
L1451: For the key secondary endpoint Take Drug Again Emax, Levene's test for homogeneity of variance was not significant (p=0.8059), the first-order-carryover effect was not significant (p=0.9096) at \alpha = 0.25, and the Shapiro-Wilk W-test on the residuals was not significant for Drug Liking Emax (p=0.10721). Therefore, the mixed effects model with period, sequence, and treatment as fixed effects and subject as a random effect was used in the reviewer's analysis.

L1470: ## 4.4. Conclusion
L1471: The primary analysis demonstrated that the mean of Drug Liking Emax scores produced by 30 mg KP415 API was significantly smaller than that by 15 mg d-MPH API with a test margin of 10 points. (p&lt;0.0001); and the median difference in maximum liking between 30 mg KP415 API and placebo was significantly smaller than 11 points (p=0.0040). The mean difference in maximum liking between 15 mg d-MPH API and placebo was significantly greater than 15 points (p&lt;0.0001). Thus, the study was validated.
L1472: The key secondary endpoint, Take Drug Again Emax, was on a unipolar visual analog scale. Unlike studies KP415-a01 and KP415-a02, in which Take Drug Again VAS was on a bipolar visual analog scale and the test margins for secondary endpoints were zero, the same test margins used for Drug Liking Emax (bipolar) were used in the analysis on this key secondary endpoint in this study. In other words, the prespecified test margins did not take unipolar scale of this measure into account. The secondary analysis on the key secondary endpoint demonstrated significantly smaller mean produced by 30 mg KP415 API than that by 15 mg d-MPH with a test margin of 10 points (p=0.0001) but failed to demonstrate no significant mean difference between 30 mg KP415 API and placebo with a test margin of 11 points (p=0.2750). However, descriptive statistics on secondary measures Any Effects VAS, Alertness/Drowsiness VAS, Bed Effects VAS, Good Effects VAS, High VAS, and Overall Drug Liking VAS and on their Emax scores all supported the findings from the primary analysis.
L1473: In conclusion, Study PK415-a03 demonstrated that with IV administration, 30 mg KP415 API has less abuse potential than 15 mg d-MPH API and did not have abuse potential compared to placebo.

L1475: ## **5.1**. **Oral Study**
L1476: ## **5.1.1. Statistical testing for High Emax**
L1477: The model considered for High Emax was a mixed effects model with period, sequence, treatment, and  first-order-carryover as fixed effects and subject as a random effect, and predose response as a covariate.  However, the covariate was not significant. The reviewer examined data. Among 45 subjects, only two  subjects' predose responses were 1 to one of five treatments, and all other predose responses were zero.  Therefore, the covariate was dropped from the model. The W-test for normality of residuals was not  significant (p= 0.7817). The Levene's test for homogeneity of variances was also not significant  (p=0.1103). However, the first-order-carryover effect was highly significant (p=0.0006). The reviewer  examined heat map by sequence for High Emax.
L1478: The heat map clearly shows that no first-order-carryover effect from any treatment to placebo except 120  mg KP415 prodrug (See blue rectangular). By examining the data, these subjects responded to 120 mg  KP415 prodrug with an Emax score of zero, and to the next treatment, placebo, with an Emax score  greater than or equal to 16. The situation is similar to what observed for Drug Liking Emax in this study.
L1479: Table 22 lists the results from the analysis on High Emax based on the mixed effects model (included the first-order-effect as a fixed effect in the model). Note that prespecified the test margins for the analyses of secondary endpoints were zero. Tests were two-sided with \alpha = 0.05 except the comparison between the test drug and placebo, in which \alpha = 0.10.

L1541: Timeline diagram illustrating the design of Study KP415.E01, divided into four phases: Screening, Dose Optimization, Treatment Period, and Follow-Up.
Screening: Visit 1 (Days -49 to -1), Visit 2 (Day 0, Open Label Baseline).
Dose Optimization: Visit 3 (Day 7, Dose Adjustment), Visit 4 (Day 14, Dose Adjustment). Activities include Practice Lab Classroom Day and Dose Adjustment.
Treatment Period: Visit 5 (Day 21, Randomization). Randomization occurs after a 2-Day Washout Period. The treatment phase involves alternating periods of KP415/d-MPH and Placebo, lasting 1 week each (Days 22-28).
Follow-Up: Visit 6 (Day 28, Full Lab Classroom Day, Efficacy Evaluations), Visit 7 (Day 31 \pm 2, Follow-Up).

The 80 mg Focalin XR and 60 mg Phentermine treatments show the highest peak mean response (around 75-80). The KP415 prodrug treatments (120 mg and 240 mg) show lower peak mean response (around 55-60). All treatments show a gradual decline in mean response over the 24-hour period, with the KP415 prodrug treatments maintaining a higher mean response for a longer duration compared to the placebo.

L1579: Flowchart illustrating the disposition of subjects:
Screened subjects (N=154) leads to Eligible subjects (N=94) and Screen failures (N=60).
Eligible subjects (N=94) leads to Randomized to Qualification Phase (N=68) and Not randomized to Qualification Phase (N=26).
Randomized to Qualification Phase (N=68) leads to Passed Qualification Phase (N=50), Qualification failures (N=15), and Discontinued from study (N=3).
Passed Qualification Phase (N=50) leads to Randomized to Treatment Phase (N=49) and Not randomized to Treatment Phase (N=1).
Randomized to Treatment Phase (N=49) leads to Completed the study (N=45) and Discontinued from study (N=4).

The 40 mg d-MPH API group shows the highest initial response (around 85) which rapidly declines, reaching around 50 by 12 hours. The Placebo group shows a very low response (around 50) throughout the 24 hours.

L1604: Figure 35 is a line graph showing the Mean time course profile by treatment for the ARCI Amphetamine Group (N=30) after IV administration. The X-axis represents Time (hour) from 0 to 8, and the Y-axis represents Mean response from 0 to 11.
The graph compares three treatments:
15 mg d-MPH API (Blue line): Shows a rapid increase in mean response, peaking around 5.5 at 1 hour, and then gradually decreasing, stabilizing around 3.5 after 4 hours.
30 mg KP415 API 30 mg (Orange line): Shows a moderate increase in mean response, peaking around 3.2 at 1 hour, and then gradually decreasing, stabilizing around 2.8 after 4 hours.
Placebo (Gray line): Shows a slight increase in mean response, peaking around 3.0 at 1 hour, and then gradually decreasing, stabilizing around 2.8 after 4 hours.
The 15 mg d-MPH API treatment consistently shows a significantly higher mean response compared to the other two treatments across the measured time points.
